// Connected street districts. Slot order and all arithmetic are shared by Node
// and the browser; the original plaza compiler stays on its v3 random stream.
import { math as Math } from './math.js';
import { scaleCount } from './spec.js';
import { queryCatalog, rectForInst } from './compile.js';
import { obbFromRect, obbOverlap, obbCorners, obbPointDistance, rectXZ, pointInArea, toWorldXZ, rotDir, strutEnds, segPointDistance, dist, TAU } from './geometry.js';

const place = i => ({ x: i.pos[0], z: i.pos[1], rotY: i.rotY });
export function extendDistricts({ spec, catalog, rng, instances, buildings, lots, paths, area }) {
  const streets = [];
  const strip = (a, b, width, extra) => ({
    id: spec.paths.id, kind: 'path', pos: [(a[0] + b[0]) / 2, (a[1] + b[1]) / 2],
    rotY: Math.atan2(b[0] - a[0], b[1] - a[1]), scale: 1,
    params: { length: dist(a, b), width }, ...extra,
  });
  function street(name, a, b, district, width = 3.2) {
    const p = strip(a, b, width, { streetId: name, district });
    streets.push(p); paths.push(p);
    return p;
  }
  street('green-approach', [0, 5.7], [0, 18], 'plaza');
  street('south-lane', [-50, 18], [58, 18], 'residential');
  street('west-lane', [-50, 18], [-50, -58], 'residential');
  street('upper-street', [-50, -36], [46, -36], 'residential');
  street('orchard-street', [-50, -58], [46, -58], 'residential');
  street('market-street', [30, 18], [30, -58], 'market', 4);
  street('farm-track', [58, 18], [58, -38], 'farm', 2.8);
  const pathObb = p => obbFromRect(rectForInst(p, catalog), place(p));
  function clear(entry, pos, rotY, scale = 1, lot = false) {
    const pl = { x: pos[0], z: pos[1], rotY };
    const o = obbFromRect(lot ? entry.lot || rectXZ(entry.bounds) : rectXZ(entry.bounds), pl, scale);
    if (obbCorners(o).some(c => "'!pointInArea(area, c, 1))) return false;
    if (obbPointDistance(o, [0, 0]) < spec.plaza.ring.radius + spec.plaza.ring.width / 2 + 1) return false;
    if (lots.some(l => obbOverlap(o, l, 0.7))) return false;
    if (paths.some(p => obbOverlap(o, pathObb(p), 0.4))) return false;
    return true;
  }
  function addBuilding(id, pos, rotY, road, kind, district) {
    const entry = catalog.get(id);
    const snap = entry.pathSnaps[0];
    const a = toWorldXZ([snap.pos[0], snap.pos[2]], { x: pos[0], z: pos[1], rotY });
    const [A, B] = strutEnds(road);
    const end = A[0] === B[0] ? [A[0], a[1]] : [a[0], A[1]];
    const dir = rotDir([snap.dir[0], snap.dir[2]], rotY);
    const p = strip(a, end, spec.paths.width, { lot: buildings.length, district });
    if (!clear(entry, pos, rotY, 1, true)) return false;
    if (lots.some(l => obbOverlap(pathObb(p), l, 0.2))) return false;
    const lot = obbFromRect(entry.lot || entry.footprint, { x: pos[0], z: pos[1], rotY });
    const inst = { id, kind, pos, rotY, scale: 1, lot: buildings.length, district, street: road.streetId };
    const b = { entry, pos, rotY, index: buildings.length, kind, lot, inst, dp: { a, end, dir, bend: 0 } };
    buildings.push(b); lots.push(lot); instances.push(inst); paths.push(p);
    return true;
  }
  const road = id => streets.find(p => p.streetId === id);
  const cozyHomes = queryCatalog(catalog, spec.houses.pick);
  const [lo, hi] = scaleCount(spec.houses.count, spec.density.houses);
  const target = rng.int(lo, hi);
  const current = buildings.filter(b => b.kind === '"'house').length;
  const streetCount = Math.max(0, target - current - 2);
  const slots = [];
  // Interleave rows so every seed populates both residential neighbourhoods.
  for (const x of [-40, -26, -12, 2, 16, 42]) {
    slots.push([[x, -46], 'upper-street', 0], [[x, -68], 'orchard-street', 0]);
  }
  for (const z of [-6, -22, -42, -58]) slots.push([[-61, z], 'west-lane', Math.PI / 2]);
  for (const [pos, name, yaw] of slots.slice(0, streetCount)) {
    addBuilding(rng.pick(cozyHomes).id, pos, yaw, road(name), 'house', 'residential');
  }
  // The farm stays at source scale, with a track-facing farmhouse and cottage.
  addBuilding('farm-farmhouse', [71, -30], -Math.PI / 2, road('farm-track'), 'house', 'farm');
  addBuilding('farm-farmhand-cottage', [69, -12], -Math.PI / 2, road('farm-track'), 'house', 'farm');
  const civics = ['cv-blacksmith', 'cv-market-hall', 'cv-windmill', 'cv-store'];
  for (let i = 0; i < civics.length; i++) addBuilding(civics[i], [42, -27 + i * 13], -Math.PI / 2, road('market-street'), 'landmark', 'market');
  addBuilding('farm-produce-stand', [66, 4], -Math.PI / 2, road('farm-track'), 'landmark', 'farm');

  // All dressing uses full mesh bounds for rejection, including roof/canopy
  // overhangs. Keep collisions conservative rather than hiding them in checks.
  const occupied = instances.filter(i => catalog.has(i.id) && "'!['"'path', 'ring'].includes(i.kind))
    .map(i => obbFromRect(rectXZ(catalog.get(i.id).bounds), place(i), i.scale));
  function dress(id, pos, rotY = 0, kind = 'prop', district = 'residential', scale = 1) {
    const e = catalog.get(id);
    if ("'!clear(e, pos, rotY, scale)) return false;
    const o = obbFromRect(rectXZ(e.bounds), { x: pos[0], z: pos[1], rotY }, scale);
    if (occupied.some(b => obbOverlap(o, b, 0.35))) return false;
    if (kind === '"'tree' || kind === 'bush') {
      const rad = Math.max(e.footprint.max[0] - e.footprint.min[0], e.footprint.max[1] - e.footprint.min[1]) / 2 * scale;
      if (paths.some(p => { const [a, b] = strutEnds(p); return segPointDistance(a, b, pos) < p.params.width / 2 + rad + 0.4; })) return false;
    }
    occupied.push(o);
    instances.push({ id, kind, pos, rotY, scale, district });
    return true;
  }
  // Market counters face the street (+X after canonicalising their -Z front).
  for (let z = -24; z <= 12; z += 6) dress('cv-market-stall', [24, z], Math.PI / 2, 'prop', 'market');
  for (const [id, pos] of [
    ['cv-flower-cart', [24, 24]], ['cv-hand-cart', [35, 24]], ['cv-horse-cart', [46, 24]],
    ['cv-notice-board', [10, 12]], ['cv-directional-post', [-5, 12]], ['cv-village-entrance-sign', [-48, 24]],
    ['farm-big-red-barn', [84, -27]], ['farm-stable', [84, -12]],
    ['farm-chicken-coop', [68, 25]], ['farm-greenhouse', [78, 22]],
    ['farm-grain-silo', [91, 7]], ['farm-hay-barn', [86, 20]],
    ['farm-windmill-pump', [64, -43]], ['farm-root-cellar', [82, -42]],
    ['farm-scarecrow', [68, -48]], ['farm-water-trough', [72, 15]],
    ['nat-log-bench', [-38, 25]], ['nat-granite-rock-cluster', [-45, 29]],
    ['nat-fallen-log-mossy', [-38, -81]],
  ]) dress(id, pos, 0, 'prop', pos[0] > 58 ? 'farm' : 'market');
  for (let x = 65; x <= 85; x += 6) for (let z = -65; z <= -49; z += 6) {
    dress(rng.pick(['farm-crop-row-field', 'farm-pumpkin-patch', 'farm-sunflower-row', 'farm-corn-stalk-cluster', 'farm-wheat-cluster']), [x, z], 0, 'prop', 'farm');
  }
  for (let x = 65; x <= 85; x += 7) dress('farm-orchard-row', [x, -73], 0, 'prop', 'farm');
  for (let z = -10; z <= 32; z += 4.1) {
    dress('farm-rail-fence', [94, z], Math.PI / 2, 'prop', 'farm');
    dress('farm-drystone-wall', [63, z], Math.PI / 2, 'prop', 'farm');
  }
  for (const p of streets) {
    const [a, b] = strutEnds(p), length = dist(a, b), d = [(b[0] - a[0]) / length, (b[1] - a[1]) / length];
    for (let t = 5; t < length - 2; t += 12) {
      const pos = [a[0] + d[0] * t + d[1] * 3, a[1] + d[1] * t - d[0] * 3];
      dress('cv-streetlamp', pos, 0, 'prop', p.district);
      dress('cv-bench', [pos[0] + d[0] * 4, pos[1] + d[1] * 4], p.rotY + Math.PI / 2, 'prop', p.district);
    }
  }
  function scatter(cfg, kind, mult, sample, district) {
    const list = queryCatalog(catalog, cfg.pick);
    const n = rng.int(...scaleCount(cfg.count, mult));
    for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) for (let t = 0; t < spec.retries.placement; t++) {
      const e = rng.pick(list), pos = sample();
      if (dress(e.id, pos, rng.float(0, TAU), kind, district, kind === 'prop' ? 1 : rng.float(0.9, 1.1))) break;
    }
  }
  const forest = () => { const a = rng.float(0, TAU), r = rng.float(35, 101); return [Math.cos(a) * r, Math.sin(a) * r]; };
  // Keep tall foreground trees away from the camera-facing open green.
  const trees = () => { const p = forest(); return p[0] + p[1] > 45 ? [-p[0], -p[1]] : p; };
  scatter(spec.trees, 'tree', spec.density.trees, trees, 'woodland');
  scatter(spec.bushes, 'bush', spec.density.bushes, forest, 'woodland');
  const market = () => [rng.float(17, 48), rng.float(-30, 29)];
  const residential = () => [rng.float(-70, 20), rng.float(-76, 26)];
  const farmyard = () => [rng.float(64, 93), rng.float(-44, 33)];
  scatter(spec.props, 'prop', spec.density.props, () => rng.chance(0.5) ? market() : residential(), 'dressing');
  scatter({ count: [35, 45], pick: { style: 'farm-dressing' } }, 'prop', spec.density.props, farmyard, 'farm');
  scatter({ count: [12, 18], pick: { style: 'animal' } }, 'prop', 1, farmyard, 'farm');
  scatter({ count: [5, 7], pick: { style: 'orchard', category: 'tree' } }, 'tree', 1, () => [rng.float(65, 90), rng.float(-85, -78)], 'farm');
  // Nature is deliberately sparse: its uncompressed meshes cost far more.
  for (const id of ['nat-oak', 'nat-oak', 'nat-wildflower', 'nat-wildflower', 'nat-lichen-granite-boulder']) {
    for (let t = 0; t < spec.retries.placement; t++) if (dress(id, trees(), 0, id === 'nat-oak' ? 'tree' : 'prop', 'woodland')) break;
  }
}

/bin/bash -lc "cat > src/compiler/batches.js <<'EOF'
import * as THREE from 'three';

// Flatten each template mesh (including its ink hull) into a draw batch.
// Nested source transforms are baked into matrices, so child hulls and native
// scale/yaw offsets keep exactly the same placement as loader.place().
export function batchInstances(template, instances) {
  template.updateMatrixWorld(true);
  const group = new THREE.Group();
  group.name = "'`batch:${template.name}`;
  group.userData.instances = instances;
  const transform = new THREE.Object3D(), matrix = new THREE.Matrix4();
  template.traverse(source => {
    if (!source.isMesh) return;
    const batch = new THREE.InstancedMesh(source.geometry, source.material, instances.length);
    batch.name = source.userData.outline ? '"'ink-batch' : template.name;
    batch.userData = { ...source.userData, instances };
    batch.castShadow = source.castShadow;
    batch.receiveShadow = source.receiveShadow;
    batch.renderOrder = source.renderOrder;
    batch.layers.mask = source.layers.mask;
    for (let i = 0; i < instances.length; i++) {
      const inst = instances[i];
      transform.position.set(inst.pos[0], 0, inst.pos[1]);
      transform.rotation.set(0, inst.rotY, 0);
      transform.scale.setScalar(inst.scale || 1);
      transform.updateMatrix();
      batch.setMatrixAt(i, matrix.multiplyMatrices(transform.matrix, source.matrixWorld));
    }
    batch.instanceMatrix.needsUpdate = true;
    batch.computeBoundingBox();
    batch.computeBoundingSphere();
    group.add(batch);
  });
  // Lightweight pivots keep the existing anchor/debug API and per-instance
  // camera bounds. Raycasts use batch.userData.instances[hit.instanceId].
  for (const inst of instances) {
    const pivot = new THREE.Group();
    pivot.position.set(inst.pos[0], 0, inst.pos[1]);
    pivot.rotation.y = inst.rotY;
    pivot.scale.setScalar(inst.scale || 1);
    pivot.userData = { inst, entry: template.userData.entry, batchPivot: true };
    group.add(pivot);
  }
  return group;
}

# Cel Town

A tiny, interactive **cel-shaded village** built with Vite + Three.js. A cozy
hamlet of four cream cottages with terracotta roofs and teal trim, gathered
around a village green with a stone well, dirt paths, rail fences, puffy trees,
rolling hills, and drifting clouds.

Everything you see is **authored in code** — composed primitives and procedural
geometry with `MeshToonMaterial` plus inverted-hull ink outlines. No textures,
no image mapping, no art packs.

## Run it

```bash
cd /workspace/cel-town
npm install
npm run dev
```

Then open the printed URL (defaults to `http://localhost:5173/`). The dev server
binds to `0.0.0.0`, so it's reachable from outside the container too.

## Controls

- **Left-drag** — orbit the camera around the hamlet
- **Scroll / pinch** — zoom in and out (clamped so you can't clip underground)
- **Right-drag** — pan

The camera starts pulled back on a high three-quarter angle so the whole town is
visible at once, with the polar angle limited so you can't go underground or
first-person. The scene stays up and interactive — there's no cinematic loop.

## The look

- **Toon shading** via a 3-band gradient ramp → strict flat color fills with
  sharp shadow bands (no soft gradients, no grain).
- **Ink outlines** via a back-faced inverted hull pushed along vertex normals —
  stable and camera-independent.
- **One directional sun** + a low sky/ground fill, with two-tone cast shadows.
- Palette pulled from the reference: cream walls, terracotta roofs, teal trim,
  lime/forest foliage, warm dirt paths, blue sky.

## Files

- `index.html` — canvas host + a small HUD.
- `src/toon.js` — palette, toon gradient ramp, toon-material factory, and the
  inverted-hull outline helper.
- `src/builders.js` — all procedural geometry: houses, trees, bushes, well,
  fences, ground, dirt paths, hills, sky dome, clouds.
- `src/main.js` — scene, lighting, camera + OrbitControls, town layout, loop.
- `vite.config.js` — dev server bound to `0.0.0.0:5173`.

## Note

`style-ref.jpg` is a **mood/layout reference only** (palette, composition). It is
never texture-mapped or draped onto any mesh — all visible art is code-authored.

## Pipelines

The broader town and simulation pipelines (cel-shaded layers, WorldX, Village Ledger, and lessons from the pixel towns) live in [`/workspace/docs/PIPELINES.md`](../docs/PIPELINES.md).

## Kit + catalog (Nov 11, 2026)

- `/kit.html` — kit viewer: every catalog entry with toggleable anchors (keys 1–6, L labels).
- `src/catalog/` — schema, entries, computed bounds, loader, debug gizmos.
- `npm run catalog:bounds` after adding/changing an entry; `npm run build && npm run shots` for headless renders in `shots/`.
- Licensed Cozy Village GLBs live in `public/licensed/` (gitignored). They are copied into `dist/` by `vite build` — never publish `dist/` to a public repo.

## Compiler v1

- `specs/hamlet.json` — town spec (format in `src/compiler/spec.js`).
- `/compile.html?seed=N` — compiles in the browser, renders, shows the check report, New seed button.
- `npm run compile -- --seed N` → `out/town-N.json`; `npm run compile -- --sweep 50` → pass rate; `npm run test:checks` → negative tests.
- `npm run test:parity` compares full town JSON from plain Node and Vite SSR
  using the browser's actual catalog loader, imported JSON and spec validation
  for seeds 1–30 plus 1234, then repeats in reverse order to catch shared-state
  mutation. It also simulates one-ulp native math differences across engines.
  Symmetric building slots use an explicit lower-angle-first tie break, and
  compiler geometry uses shared arithmetic implementations of trigonometry
  and vector lengths so native engine roundoff cannot change the town JSON
  or reorder slots and change RNG draws.
  This runs without opening a server port or launching a renderer; it does not
  replace a Chrome rendering check.

## Compiler v2

- Plaza ring (`makePlazaRing`), continuous gated fences, landmarks (`cv-bakery`, `cv-tavern`, `cv-chapel`, `cv-store`), `density` knobs.
- `/?seed=N` renders a compiled seed on the main page; `/compile.html?seed=N&debug=1` opens with anchors on.

## Post-process look (visual system v2)

A render-target chain in `src/post/`:

1. Scene.
2. Normal/depth prepass.
3. Hull ink layer.
4. Kuwahara (r=6).
5. Hull composite.
6. Depth and normal ink.
7. sRGB grade with a 0.3 palette snap.
8. Output.

Every stage can be toggled in the bottom-right Look panel or by query string:
`?post=0`, `edges`, `normals`, `kuwahara=0..7`, `grade`, `quant=0..1`,
`hull`, `inkWidth`, `inkMul`, `inkMix`, `normalWeight`, `depthThresh`.

`?cam=iso` gives a tight 2:1 orthographic camera.

`npm run shots:post` renders before/after pairs into `shots/post-*`, and
`?perf=1` sets `window.__perf`.

The ideas come from jankeesvw/isometric-helenaveen. That repo has no licence,
so none of its code is used.

## Compiler v3

- **Gates**: `makeGate` builder (catalog `b-gate`, category `gate`). Every fenced
  lot gets `{ id:'b-gate', kind:'gate', pos, rotY, scale:1, lot, edge:'front', width, open }`
  in its front gap, centred where the door path crosses the front fence line.
  Spec: `fences.gate { id, open }` (default open 0.55, lowered per lot so leaves
  clear the house). `gate: null` = no gates and no fenced lots; a gate id missing
  from the catalog or not category `gate` is a spec error.
- **Cobble ring**: `b-plaza-ring` now draws mortar, 4 rows of irregular cobbles
  and inner/outer kerbs as one vertex-coloured mesh (~11k tris). `params.style:'flat'`
  gives the old look.
- **Facing**: door yaws blend from plaza-facing toward the camera at the arc ends,
  capped by `houses.facing { maxCameraAngle:65, maxPlazaAngle:50, blend:0.8, maxPathBend:8 }`.
  Each house/landmark records `facing { cameraAngle, plazaAngle }` (degrees,
  against `town.camera.pos`). Door paths run straight out of the door onto the ring.
- **Ink**: `?inkFade=0` turns off the distance fade (edge ink and hull thickness
  fall to 35% at twice the core depth). `?foliageMask=0` brings back crease ink
  inside tree crowns. Both are also in the Look panel.
- **Framing**: `stage.setPerspectiveFit(townCoreBoxes(scene), { panelFrac, fill })`
  fits the built core with the real perspective camera (0.86 fill; right of a
  20% panel on `/compile.html`, centred on `/?seed=N`). `?cam=x,y,z` still wins.
- `npm run compile -- --stats N` prints worst facing angles, gate counts and alignment.

google-chrome-stable

// Post-process chain for the cel town. Toggleable; ?post=0 falls back to a
// plain renderer.render identical to the baseline. Inspired by
// jankeesvw/isometric-helenaveen (ideas only — that repo has no licence, so no
// code from it is used here).
//
// CHAIN (device-pixel render targets, linear light until the final pass):
//   1. scene  -> sceneRT  (colour; MSAA x4; own depth renderbuffer)
//   2. prepass-> prepassRT (MeshNormalMaterial, inverted-hull outlines hidden;
//      carries a DepthTexture) — gives CLEAN depth + view-space normals for the
//      ink, so ink comes from real geometry, not the hulls. Only when
//      edges/normals are on.
//   3. Kuwahara (radius uKuwahara) : painterly, edge-preserving smoothing.
//   4. Edge ink : darken the near side of depth steps (multiply by ~0.58, a la
//      helenaveen) + normal discontinuities; depth threshold is relative for
//      perspective, absolute for ortho.
//   5. Grade : optional palette quantise + saturation/contrast/warm/vignette.
//   6. Output : linear -> sRGB to the screen.
//
// DEFAULT PARAMS (tuned on shots/post-*, see options.js DEFAULTS):
//   edges on, normals OFF (?normals=1 to opt in), kuwahara r=3 with ink
//   protection, grade on (sat 1.06, contrast 1.05, warm 0.02, vignette 0.14,
//   done in sRGB space), quant 0 (off), hull ON (inverted hulls give the bold
//   silhouette; post depth ink adds a softer inner line). inkMul 0.55,
//   depthThresh 0.03 (relative, persp) / 0.6 (ortho), inkWidth 1.2.
import * as THREE from 'three';
import { FullScreenQuad } from 'three/addons/postprocessing/Pass.js';
//
// v3 ink polish:
//   * inkFade (default on, ?inkFade=0 off): in perspective the screen-space
//     ink strength and width, and the inverted-hull thickness (toon.js
//     HULL_FADE), are full up to the town-core depth (where the view axis hits
//     the ground) and fall linearly to 35% at twice that depth.
//   * foliageMask (default on, ?foliageMask=0 off): meshes flagged
//     userData.foliage (tree/bush crowns, builder puffs) are also put on
//     FOLIAGE_LAYER; after the normal prepass they are redrawn (depth equal)
//     with alpha 0.5, so the normal target's alpha marks foliage. The edge
//     pass then skips crease (normal) ink between two foliage pixels; depth
//     (silhouette) ink on crowns is unchanged.
import { DEFAULTS } from './options.js';
import { HULL_FADE } from '../toon.js';
import { kuwaharaMaterial, edgeMaterial, gradeMaterial, outputMaterial, hullCompositeMaterial } from './shaders.js';

// Inverted-hull outline meshes live on their own layer so the post chain can
// draw them AFTER the Kuwahara pass (otherwise the filter eats the thin ink).
export const HULL_LAYER = 1;
// Foliage meshes (also on layer 0) for the prepass foliage-flag redraw.
export const FOLIAGE_LAYER = 2;
const FADE_MIN = 0.35;

const MSAA = 4; // colour-RT multisampling (WebGL2; sceneRT has no depth texture)

export function createPost(renderer, scene, camera, opts = {}) {
  const options = { ...DEFAULTS, ...opts };
  const size = new THREE.Vector2();
  renderer.getDrawingBufferSize(size);

  const colorRT = (samples) => new THREE.WebGLRenderTarget(size.x, size.y, {
    type: THREE.HalfFloatType, // linear HDR-ish working buffer, no banding
    depthBuffer: true, samples,
  });
  const sceneRT = colorRT(MSAA);
  const rtA = colorRT(0);
  const rtB = colorRT(0);
  // Hull ink layer: scene depth (depth-only) + hulls on top, alpha = coverage.
  const hullRT = new THREE.WebGLRenderTarget(size.x, size.y, { type: THREE.UnsignedByteType, depthBuffer: true, samples: MSAA });
  const depthOnly = new THREE.MeshBasicMaterial({ colorWrite: false });
  const hullComp = hullCompositeMaterial();
  const _clear = new THREE.Color();

  // Prepass target carries a depth texture (clean depth for the ink) + normals.
  const depthTexture = new THREE.DepthTexture(size.x, size.y, THREE.UnsignedIntType);
  const prepassRT = new THREE.WebGLRenderTarget(size.x, size.y, { type: THREE.UnsignedByteType, depthBuffer: true, depthTexture });

  const normalMat = new THREE.MeshNormalMaterial();
  // Alpha 0.5 flags foliage. NoBlending (not NormalBlending) so three does not
  // define OPAQUE (which forces alpha to 1) and the alpha is written as-is; a
  // small polygon offset makes the equal-depth redraw reliably pass.
  const foliageNormalMat = new THREE.MeshNormalMaterial({
    opacity: 0.5, blending: THREE.NoBlending, polygonOffset: true, polygonOffsetFactor: -1, polygonOffsetUnits: -1,
  });
  const _fwd = new THREE.Vector3();
  const kuwahara = kuwaharaMaterial();
  const edge = edgeMaterial();
  const grade = gradeMaterial();
  const output = outputMaterial();
  const quad = new FullScreenQuad();

  function setHullVisible(v) {
    scene.traverse((o) => {
      if (o.userData && o.userData.outline) { o.visible = v; o.layers.set(HULL_LAYER); }
      else if (o.userData && o.userData.foliage) o.layers.enable(FOLIAGE_LAYER);
    });
  }

# Full-Story Verification

You are a verification orchestrator. Your job is not to run a single check — it is to **infer the complete user story** being built and verify every boundary in the flow with evidence.

Your focus is the **end-to-end story**, not any single layer.

## When This Triggers

- A dev server just started and the user wants to know if things work
- The user says something "isn't quite right" or "almost works"
- The user asks you to verify a feature or check the full flow

## Step 1 — Infer the User Story

Before checking anything, determine **what is being built**:

1. Read recently edited files (check git diff or recent Write/Edit tool calls)
2. Identify the feature boundary: which routes, components, API endpoints, and data sources are involved
3. Scan `package.json` scripts, route structure (`app/` or `pages/`), and environment files (`.env*`)
4. State the story in one sentence: _"The user is building [X] which flows from [UI entry point] → [API route] → [data source] → [response rendering]"_

**Do not skip this step.** Every subsequent check must be anchored to the inferred story.

## Step 2 — Establish Evidence Baseline

Gather the current state across all layers:

| Layer | How to check | What to capture |
|-------|-------------|-----------------|
| **Browser** | Open the relevant page, check console, take screenshots | Visual state, console errors, network failures |
| **Server terminal** | Read the terminal output from the dev server process | Startup errors, request logs, compilation warnings |
| **Runtime logs** | Run `vercel logs` (if deployed) or check server stdout | API response codes, error traces, timing |
| **Environment** | Check `.env.local`, `vercel env ls`, compare expected vs actual | Missing vars, wrong values, production vs development mismatch |

Report what you find at each layer before proceeding. Use this reporting contract:

> **Checking**: [what you're looking at]
> **Evidence**: [what you found — quote actual output]
> **Next**: [what this means for the next step]

## Step 3 — Walk the Data Flow

Trace the feature's data path from trigger to completion:

1. **UI trigger** — What user action initiates the flow? (button click, page load, form submit)
2. **Client → Server** — What request is made? Check the fetch/action call, verify the URL, method, and payload match the API route
3. **API route handler** — Read the route file. Does it handle the method? Does it validate input? Does it call the right service/database?
4. **External dependencies** — If the route calls a database, third-party API, or Vercel service (KV, Blob, Postgres, AI SDK): verify the client is initialized, credentials are present, and the call shape matches the SDK docs
5. **Response → UI** — Does the response format match what the client expects? Is error handling present on both sides?

At each boundary, check for these common breaks:
- **Missing `await`** on async operations
- **Wrong HTTP method** (GET handler but POST fetch)
- **Env var absent** in runtime but present in `.env.local`
- **Import mismatch** (server module imported in client component or vice versa)
- **Type mismatch** between API response and client expectation
- **Missing error boundary** — unhandled rejection crashes the page silently

## Step 4 — Report With Evidence

Summarize findings in a structured report:

```
## Verification Report: [Feature Name]

**Story**: [one-sentence description of the user story]

### Flow Status
| Boundary | Status | Evidence |
|----------|--------|----------|
| UI renders | ✅/❌ | [screenshot or console output] |
| Client → API | ✅/❌ | [request/response or error] |
| API → Data | ✅/❌ | [log output or error trace] |
| Data → Response | ✅/❌ | [response shape or error] |
| Response → UI | ✅/❌ | [rendered output or error] |

### Issues Found
1. [Issue]: [evidence] → [fix]

### Verified Working
- [What was confirmed working with evidence]
```

## Stop Conditions

**Stop verifying when**:
- All boundaries in the flow are confirmed working with evidence — report success
- You find the **first broken boundary** — report it with evidence and a specific fix, do not continue past the break
- Two consecutive layers return no useful signal (e.g., no logs, no errors, no output) — flag the observability gap and recommend adding logging before continuing

**Do not**:
- Run the same check more than twice
- Continue past a confirmed broken boundary
- Verify unrelated features — stay on the inferred story
- Spend time on cosmetic issues (styling, spacing) unless the user specifically asked

## Suggest Verification After Implementation

When you finish building or implementing a feature (wrote code, created routes, set up a project), briefly let the user know they can ask you to verify everything works — e.g. browser verification or end-to-end flow check. One sentence is enough. Don't force it if only a small fix or question was involved.

// Seeded RNG (mulberry32) + a few drawing helpers. Deterministic: the same
// seed always yields the same stream, so a town is reproducible from spec+seed.

export function mulberry32(seed) {
  let a = seed >>> 0;
  return function () {
    a |= 0; a = (a + 0x6d2b79f5) | 0;
    let t = Math.imul(a ^ (a >>> 15), 1 | a);
    t = (t + Math.imul(t ^ (t >>> 7), 61 | t)) ^ t;
    return ((t ^ (t >>> 14)) >>> 0) / 4294967296;
  };
}

/** A small random API over one mulberry32 stream. */
export function makeRng(seed) {
  const r = mulberry32(seed);
  return {
    next: r,
    float: (a = 0, b = 1) => a + (b - a) * r(),
    // inclusive integer in [a, b]
    int: (a, b) => a + Math.floor(r() * (b - a + 1)),
    pick: (arr) => arr[Math.floor(r() * arr.length)],
    chance: (p) => r() < p,
    sign: () => (r() < 0.5 ? -1 : 1),
  };
}

/** Derive a distinct sub-seed from a base seed + salt (for town retries). */
export function deriveSeed(seed, salt) {
  let h = ((seed >>> 0) ^ Math.imul((salt | 0) + 1, 0x9e3779b1)) >>> 0;
  h = Math.imul(h ^ (h >>> 16), 0x45d9f3b);
  h = Math.imul(h ^ (h >>> 16), 0x45d9f3b);
  return (h ^ (h >>> 16)) >>> 0;
}

// Turn a compiled town into three.js objects. Browser-only (imports the loader
// and builders). buildTown(town, scene) adds the world (sky/hills/ground),
// the plaza disk and every instance, resolving async mesh loads before the
// returned Promise settles.
import * as THREE from 'three';
import { batchInstances } from './batches.js';
import { place, instantiate, RESOLVED } from '../catalog/loader.js';
import { PALETTE, toonMaterial } from '../toon.js';
import { makeGround, makeHills, makeSky } from '../builders.js';

function plazaDisk(radius) {
  const disk = new THREE.Mesh(new THREE.CircleGeometry(radius, 48), toonMaterial(PALETTE.grassDark));
  disk.rotation.x = -Math.PI / 2;
  disk.position.y = 0.02; // just above makeGround's green patch
  disk.receiveShadow = true;
  return disk;
}

export function buildTown(town, scene) {
  scene.add(makeSky());
  const extent = town.area.shape === 'circle' ? town.area.radius : Math.hypot(town.area.w, town.area.d) / 2;
  const groundRadius = Math.max(70, extent + 15);
  scene.add(makeHills(groundRadius / 70));
  scene.add(makeGround(groundRadius));

  const loads = [];
  const batches = new Map();
  const paramsFor = inst => inst.kind === 'gate'
    ? { ...(inst.params || {}), ...(inst.width != null ? { width: inst.width } : {}), ...(inst.open != null ? { open: inst.open } : {}) }
    : inst.params;
  const batchKey = inst => `${inst.id}:${JSON.stringify(paramsFor(inst) || {})}`;
  if (town.instances.some(i => i.street)) {
    for (const inst of town.instances) {
      if (!RESOLVED.has(inst.id) || !['tree', 'bush', 'fence', 'gate', 'prop'].includes(inst.kind)) continue;
      const key = batchKey(inst);
      if (!batches.has(key)) batches.set(key, []);
      batches.get(key).push(inst);
    }
    for (const [key, list] of batches) {
      if (list.length < 2) { batches.delete(key); continue; }
      loads.push(instantiate(list[0].id, { params: paramsFor(list[0]) }).then(template => { const batch = batchInstances(template, list); scene.add(batch); return batch; }));
    }
  }
  for (const inst of town.instances) {
    if (inst.kind === 'plaza') { scene.add(plazaDisk(inst.radius)); continue; }
    if (batches.has(batchKey(inst))) continue;
    const [x, z] = inst.pos;
    // Gates carry their gap width / swing as top-level instance fields.
    const params = paramsFor(inst);
    loads.push(
      place(inst.id, { x, z, rotY: inst.rotY, params, scale: inst.scale || 1 })
        .then((obj) => { obj.userData.inst = inst; scene.add(obj); return obj; }),
    );
  }
  return Promise.all(loads);
}
// Headless screenshots of the built pages (run `npm run build` first).
// Serves dist/ with vite preview, renders in system Chrome via SwiftShader.
//   node tools/shots.mjs [name=url-path ...]
import { chromium } from 'playwright-core';
import { preview } from 'vite';
import { mkdirSync } from 'node:fs';

// `--post` renders before/after pairs for the post chain (seeds 1234 & 7,
// perspective + iso). Otherwise: explicit name=url-path args, or the defaults.
function postPairs() {
  const out = [];
  for (const seed of [1234, 7]) {
    for (const [tag, cam] of [['persp', ''], ['iso', '&cam=iso']]) {
      out.push([`post-${seed}-${tag}-before`, `/compile.html?seed=${seed}&post=0${cam}`]);
      out.push([`post-${seed}-${tag}-after`, `/compile.html?seed=${seed}${cam}`]);
    }
  }
  return out;
}

const rest = process.argv.slice(2);
const shots = rest.includes('--post')
  ? postPairs()
  : rest.length
    ? rest.map((a) => [a.slice(0, a.indexOf('=')), a.slice(a.indexOf('=') + 1)])
    : [['hamlet', '/'], ['kit', '/kit.html'], ['kit-clean', '/kit.html?anchors=&labels=0']];
mkdirSync(new URL('../shots/', import.meta.url), { recursive: true });

const server = await preview({ preview: { port: 0, strictPort: true, host: '127.0.0.1' } });
// Port 0 = a fresh OS-assigned port, so a stale preview server can never answer.
const BASE_URL = server.resolvedUrls.local[0].replace(/\/$/, '');
const browser = await chromium.launch({
  executablePath: process.env.CHROME || '/usr/bin/google-chrome',
  args: ['--use-gl=angle', '--use-angle=swiftshader', '--enable-unsafe-swiftshader', '--ignore-gpu-blocklist', '--no-sandbox'],
});
try {
  for (const [name, path] of shots) {
    const page = await browser.newPage({ viewport: { width: 1600, height: 900 } });
    const errs = [];
    page.on('console', (m) => { if (m.type() === 'error' || m.type() === 'warning') errs.push(m.text()); });
    page.on('pageerror', (e) => errs.push(String(e.stack || e)));
    page.on('response', (r) => { if (r.status() >= 400 && !r.url().endsWith('favicon.ico')) errs.push(r.status() + ' ' + r.url()); });
    await page.goto(BASE_URL + path);
    try {
      await page.waitForFunction(() => window.__ready === true, null, { timeout: 20000 });
    } catch (e) {
      console.error(`${name}: scene never became ready\n  ` + errs.join('\n  '));
      throw e;
    }
    await page.waitForTimeout(1500);
    const file = new URL(`../shots/${name}.png`, import.meta.url).pathname;
    await page.screenshot({ path: file });
    console.log('shot', file, errs.length ? '\n  console: ' + errs.join('\n  ') : '');
    await page.close();
  }
} finally {
  await browser.close();
  server.httpServer.close();
}

/bin/bash -lc "cat > tools/check-batches.mjs <<'EOF'
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import * as THREE from 'three';
import { batchInstances } from '../src/compiler/batches.js';
import { makeTree, makeGate } from '../src/builders.js';

for (const build of [() => makeTree({ scale: 1.2, seed: 3 }), () => makeGate({ width: 2.9, open: 0.55 })]) {
  const template = new THREE.Group();
  template.name = 'template';
  template.userData.entry = { bounds: { min: [-2, 0, -2], max: [2, 5, 2] } };
  const content = build();
  content.scale.setScalar(1.6);
  content.rotation.y = Math.PI;
  content.position.y = -0.2;
  template.add(content);
  const instances = [
    { pos: [9, -4], rotY: 0.8, scale: 1 },
    { pos: [-12, 7], rotY: -1.4, scale: 0.7 },
    { pos: [3, 16], rotY: 2.9, scale: 1.15 },
  ];
  const originals = new THREE.Group();
  for (const i of instances) {
    const copy = template.clone(true);
    copy.position.set(i.pos[0], 0, i.pos[1]); copy.rotation.y = i.rotY; copy.scale.setScalar(i.scale);
    originals.add(copy);
  }
  const batched = batchInstances(template, instances);
  const expected = new THREE.Box3().setFromObject(originals);
  const actual = new THREE.Box3().setFromObject(batched);
  assert(expected.min.distanceTo(actual.min) < 1e-5 && expected.max.distanceTo(actual.max) < 1e-5, 'batches preserve world extents of nested geometry / ink');
  const sourceMeshes = [];
  template.traverse(o => { if (o.isMesh) sourceMeshes.push(o); });
  const batches = batched.children.filter(o => o.isInstancedMesh);
  assert.equal(batches.length, sourceMeshes.length, 'one draw per template mesh, independent of copy count');
  for (const [i, b] of batches.entries()) {
    assert.equal(b.count, instances.length);
    assert.equal(b.geometry, sourceMeshes[i].geometry);
    assert.equal(b.material, sourceMeshes[i].material);
    assert.equal(b.userData.outline, sourceMeshes[i].userData.outline);
    assert.equal(b.userData.foliage, sourceMeshes[i].userData.foliage);
    assert.equal(b.userData.instances, instances);
  }
  const pivots = batched.children.filter(o => o.userData.batchPivot);
  assert.equal(pivots.length, instances.length);
  assert(pivots.every((p, i) => p.userData.inst === instances[i] && p.userData.entry === template.userData.entry));
}
console.log('batches: tree and gate geometry, ink, bounds, shared resources and per-instance metadata pass');
EOF

378:export function makeGate({ width = 2.9, open = 0.55, height = 1.22, leaves = 'auto' } = {}) {
// Adapters so builder entries can be constructed from plain JSON params.
import { makeHouse, makeTree, makeBush, makeWell, makeFence, makePath, makePlazaRing, makeGate } from '../builders.js';

export const BUILDERS = {
  makeHouse: (p) => makeHouse(p),
  makeTree: (p) => makeTree(p.scale ?? 1, p.seed ?? 0),
  makeBush: (p) => makeBush(p.scale ?? 1),
  makeWell: () => makeWell(),
  // Fence / path pieces are a straight run of `length` along local X / Z.
  makeFence: (p) => makeFence(-p.length / 2, 0, p.length / 2, 0, p.spacing ?? 2),
  makePath: (p) => makePath(0, -p.length / 2, 0, p.length / 2, p.width ?? 2.4),
  // Plaza ring — flat paved loop around the well (inner/outer world radii).
  makePlazaRing: (p) => makePlazaRing(p),
  // Fence gate — posts at x = +-width/2 on the hinge line, leaves swung `open`.
  makeGate: (p) => makeGate(p),
};

## Large preset

Open "'`/?size=large&seed=1234` or `/compile.html?size=large&seed=1234`.
The main page switches to the seeded compiler when `size=large` is present,
including without a seed. The compiler page also accepts `?spec=specs/large.json`.
With no size flag, the existing hamlet stays unchanged: the original catalog
pools are tagged `v3-kit`, and seed 1234 retains byte-identical compiled JSON.

```bash
npm run compile -- --size large --seed 1234 --out out/large/
npm run compile -- --spec specs/large.json --sweep 50 --out out/large/
npm run test:checks
npm run test:parity
```

`specs/large.json` requests 18–22 houses and 7 civic buildings, arranged around
a retained plaza core, two residential streets, a market street and a farm
track. Higher house density adds residential rows. Door paths join a connected
street graph leading to the plaza. Plaza lots retain their camera/plaza facing
caps; other buildings must face their assigned street. Fenced dwellings retain
the same gate, clearance and continuity checks.

New catalog entries include Cozy blacksmith/market hall/windmill, stalls,
carts, lamps and dressing; Farm dwellings, agricultural structures, crops,
animals and fences; and sparse Nature trees, rocks and flowers. Cozy uses
scale 1.6; Farm and Nature use native scale 1.0. Market stalls have their actual
-Z counter front normalised with a yaw offset. Farm structures without verified
doors are props and are never selected as dwellings.

Repeated meshes and gates are instanced with shared geometry/materials,
including their ink hulls. Debug pivots retain each `userData.inst`; raycasts
can look up `mesh.userData.instances[hit.instanceId]`. Ground radius and hills
grow with the compiled area. Nature pieces remain sparse to limit geometry.
`?perf=1` still enables later runtime measurements. Seed sweep, counts and
verification details are in `out/codex-large.md` and `out/large/sweep.json`.
'"'''

The original default view and seeded hamlet remain unchanged. Original catalog pools now carry `v3-kit`; seed 1234 is byte-identical to the saved pre-change JSON. Its frozen SHA-256 regression is checked by `test:parity`. No hamlet counts changed.

## Seed 1234 counts

| Measure | Default hamlet | Large |
|---|---:|---:|
| Building lots | 6 | 27 |
| houses | 5 | 20 |
| landmarks | 1 | 7 |
| paths | 6 | 34 |
| fences | 41 | 109 |
| trees | 15 | 91 |
| bushes | 7 | 44 |
| props | 3 | 337 |
| instances | 85 | 656 |

Large has 4.5× as many building lots, including two farm dwellings. Farm outbuildings, market stalls, crops and animals are counted as props, not dwelling lots. Default seed 1234 passes on attempt 0; large passes on attempt 1. Neither has framing warnings. Large ground radius is 125 for area radius 110; hills expand with it.

## Layout and rendering

Retains a camera/plaza-facing core, then adds two rear residential streets, a west lane, a market street, an approach from the green and a farm track. Density can add further residential rows; checks at house density 0.7, 1.25 and 1.5 pass for seed 1234 (14, 28 and 29 houses). Door reachability follows the connected path graph to the plaza. Street buildings must face their assigned street within 20 degrees; plaza buildings retain the original 45-degree camera / 50-degree plaza caps and compiler path-bend cap. Fenced dwellings retain gates, clearance and continuity checks. New hard checks cover streets crossing lots, district streets and civic counts.

Repeated trees, bushes, fences, lamps, crops, props and gates use InstancedMesh batches with shared geometry/materials, including ink hulls. Per-instance pivots retain `userData.inst` and anchors for debug/camera fitting; raycasts use `userData.instances[hit.instanceId]`. Nature is sparse (7 instances at seed 1234: two oaks, two wildflowers, two rocks and a fallen log). The bulk dressing query explicitly selects Cozy assets; Nature cannot enter that pool.

Cozy remains scale 1.6. Farm and Nature remain scale 1.0. Detailed farmhouse vertex inspection found its door at z≈3.13 behind a porch ending at z≈4.40; the path snap is z=4.70. The market hall uses its +Z public awning as the front. Market stalls have a -Z counter, normalised with yawOffset=π. Inspection evidence is in `out/door-inspection.txt` and `out/mesh-inspection.txt`; all bounds were recomputed by `npm run catalog:bounds`.

## Verification

- `npm run test:checks`: all 13 original breaks and 8 large-town breaks caught, plus batch geometry/ink/bounds/shared-resource/metadata checks.
- `npm run test:parity`: 31 hamlet seeds and 4 large seeds, forward/reverse, through Vite SSR and simulated one-ulp engine rounding; full town JSON identical. Frozen original-hamlet checksum also passes.
- `npm run compile -- --spec specs/large.json --sweep 50 --out out/large/`: 42/50 first attempt (84%), 50/50 after retries (100%). No remaining failing hard checks. Maximum retry index 4. Full seed rows: `out/large/sweep.json`.
- `npm run build`: passes; existing large-bundle warning remains. No public assets are copied into dist. Private Vite preview serves them directly from public.
- Five local seed-1234 compilations averaged ~26 ms for hamlet and ~529 ms for large, excluding GLB loading/rendering. Samples: `out/compile-timings.json`. Logs: `out/checks.log`, `out/parity.log`, `out/build.log`.

## Known limits

No screenshots or frame-time claims. Browser verification was blocked by this environment: Vite listen returned EPERM and Chrome could not launch. Rendering and `?perf=1` measurements remain for the later pass. Farm outbuildings without verified doors are collision-checked props and do not claim door navigation. Newly catalogued road tiles/water/bridge pieces are available in kit.html; town roads use the existing procedural strips. Build previews require public assets alongside dist (handled by npm run preview); a standalone dist directory does not contain licensed GLBs.
